Theorem 215.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: In the functional framework, every convergence analysis morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 667. The resulting operator achieves bound O(117/n^5).

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to functional theory, we decompose the problem into 667 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 117. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(5/n). QED.

Theorem 215.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let the functional structure S satisfy the conditions of structural decomposition framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 121. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 725 and converges at rate O(1/n^4).

Proof: We apply the structural decomposition principle to the functional decomposition. By minimality, the element a^121 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 725-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
